Reconcile its original\n46\tkey with `remote operation --key KEY --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD`.\n47\t\n48\t## Read one revision\n49\t\n50\t```text\n51\tremote read --typed --compact --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD --artifact ID --expected-revision REVISION --context UUID --context-version N\n52\t```\n53\t\n54\tFor an imported baseline, replace the context fields with `--baseline ID`.\n55\tReads take no `--operation-key`, `--expected-project-version` or `--evaluator-file`.\n56\tChoose the exact revision and context from the selected inputs or actual results.\n57\t\n58\t## Reads and discovery\n59\t\n60\t| Need | Operation |\n61\t| --- | --- |\n62\t| Service readiness and selected components | `remote status --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD` |\n63\t| Local wheel metadata | `identity` |\n64\t| Captured calls/failures so far | `observations`; includes recovered and denied native calls, no content verdict |\n65\t| Complete released instruction sections | `instructions --section \"RESOURCE_ID#HEADING\"`; repeat for up to 12 sections |\n66\t| One unknown input location | `find-file BASENAME --under RELATIVE_DIRECTORY/` |\n67\t| One exact inventory entry | `lookup-file RELATIVE_NAME` |\n68\t| One saved JSON field | `read-json ABSOLUTE_FILE --pointer '\"/field\"'`; omit pointer for root; optional `--keys` |\n69\t| A raw base64 field | Add `--decode-base64` to that field read |\n70\t| One text file or independent reads together | `read-text ABSOLUTE_FILE [ABSOLUTE_FILE ...]`; up to eight selected inventoried inputs, the launcher-pinned task.md, or work files, at most 64 KiB output |\n71\t| Recover a truncated text read | `read-text ABSOLUTE_FILE --offset 0 --limit 4096`; one file, character offsets; continue from next_offset until complete is true |\n72\t\n73\tFor `instructions`, use canonical IDs such as `docs/engineering/harness/DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#read-this-when`. The earlier `released-resources/` inventory prefix also works. Request known sections together.\n74\tFor authoring checklists and the repeated released 0.22.1 continuation heading,\n75\tuse the exact selectors in the selected plugin's `change/references/hosted-drafts.md`.\n76\t\n77\tFor known paths, use them directly. Inventory names are relative to the staged\n78\tinputs directory: `released-resources/docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`,\n79\t`source/src/greeting.py`, `client-help/create-artifact.txt`. **Do not add `inputs/`\n80\tto these names.** Native Read takes the absolute path, not an inventory name.\n81\tA digest or lookup is not a content read. Batch independent reads when their exact\n82\tpaths are known. Unknown headings must not be guessed;\n83\tread the named file once to locate its actual headings. Request known current\n84\tsections together and reuse unchanged content. A full inventory is not required.\n85\t\n86\tSource inspection and test execution are different actions. Drafting a verification\n87\tcontract describes future checks; it does not require running them. If the task\n88\tauthorizes execution, use [Execution tools](native-execution-tools.md).\n89\t\n90\t## Other operations\n91\t\n92\tRaw mode remains `remote OPERATION --request ABSOLUTE_FILE --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD`.\n93\tUse it for operations without typed input, or when explicitly checking a raw\n94\tcontract. Original schemas are under `reference/server/contracts/`. Selected\n95\tschema views use `schemas/remote-v1/OPERATION.json` or `schemas/read-v1/OPERATION.json`.\n96\tEach view retains its original pointer/digest. Lifecycle test-copy schemas are\n97\tseparate; do not load them for drafting alone. `--key` reads a receipt, not a document.\n98\t\n99\tUse `observations` before the final failure report. It reads existing native event/command captures and identifies the snapshot boundary; subsequent report writes are not yet counted. Keep its paths as evidence instead of copying every command. It supplies no draft text, content judgement or lifecycle decision.\n100\t\n101\tRetain failed calls and recoveries. Template admission is not a content review.\n102\tReport only inspected facts and actual outcomes; distinguish unresolved content\n103\tfrom a completed definition. Do not claim an exhaustive failure count from memory.\n104\t"

{
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_01T773Q2KqbT5aqnZBpjvwjc",

  "content": "1\t# Artifact Authoring\n2\t\n3\tThe key words **MUST**, **MUST NOT**, **REQUIRED**, **SHALL**, **SHALL NOT**,\n4\t**SHOULD**, **SHOULD NOT**, **RECOMMENDED**, **NOT RECOMMENDED**, **MAY**, and\n5\t**OPTIONAL** in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14\n6\t(RFC 2119 and RFC 8174) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals.\n7\t\n8\t## Purpose and precedence\n9\t\n10\tThis policy guides authoring, design and review. Templates and skills point\n11\there; `harnessctl create-artifact` prints the selected type's checklist.\n12\tThe shared principles and questions are maintained here.\n13\t\n14\tIt ranks below the machine contracts, approved formal semantics, and the\n15\tworkflow, decision-right, quality-gate, and traceability policies, and above\n16\ttemplates, skills, prompts, and model prose. It grants no authority: a\n17\tchecklist item is a review standard, not an approval. Rules marked\n18\t*mechanical* are enforced by the validator or a gate; the rest are judgement\n19\trules for the accountable reviewer.\n20\t\n21\t## Design simplicity\n22\t\n23\tFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\n24\treports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\n25\tat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\n26\tor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\n27\tidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\n28\tdisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\n29\tfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\n30\tComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\n31\trequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\n32\t\n33\tChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\n34\tconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\n35\ta simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\n36\ttest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\n37\tboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\n38\timplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\n39\tsafety properties.\n40\t\n41\tStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\n42\tusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\n43\tcan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\n44\tcan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\n45\tor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\n46\tor dependencies also has a cost.\n47\t\n48\tApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\n49\tand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\n50\tfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\n51\tin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\n52\tis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\n53\tsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\n54\tarchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\n55\t\n56\tThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\n57\tmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\n58\t\n59\t## requirement\n60\t\n61\t### Checklist\n62\t\n63\t- State the observable behavior and why it is needed. Ordinary language is accepted; `SHALL` is optional.\n64\t- What agreed outcome does this obligation serve, for whom and under which conditions? Separate requirements from assumptions and speculative needs.\n65\t- Include a concrete acceptance condition in `Examples`, `Acceptance`, `measure`, `verification_notes`, or a linked verification contract.\n66\t- Name the source capability and a verification method. Supported method arrays contain `test`, `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration`; retained text methods remain readable.\n67\t- Replace unfinished placeholders and resolve blocking decisions before approval.\n68\t\n69\t### Guidance\n70\t\n71\tWrite enough for someone to implement and check the behavior. Use the template's\n72\tsections when helpful. Word counts, sentence shapes and numbers of code references\n73\tare not checked. Writing hints never block approval. Optional metadata must still\n74\thave a valid value when supplied.\n75\t\n76\t## intent\n77\t\n78\t### Checklist\n79\t\n80\t- Explain the present problem, who it affects, and the outcome wanted.\n81\t- What is the smallest useful outcome, and which needs and constraints are actually agreed?\n82\t- Say how the owner will know the change helped; an honest baseline may be `not measured`.\n83\t- State the important scope limits. Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\n84\t\n85\t## capability\n86\t\n87\t### Checklist\n88\t\n89\t- Say what an actor should be able to do and any relevant conditions.\n90\t- Does each supported case serve an agreed need under those conditions?\n91\t- Link the intent it serves. The graph lists the derived requirements.\n92\t- Put implementation details where they help the implementer; no required `can ... under` wording.\n93\t\n94\t## specification\n95\t\n96\t### Checklist\n97\t\n98\t- Explain what an implementation must do, including meaningful failure behavior.\n99\t- Is each promised behavior necessary? Could a simpler contract meet the agreed need? Prescribe a mechanism only when it is a necessary constraint.\n100\t- Link the requirements and show an example someone can check.\n101\t- Give a rule a stable identifier when evidence or a decision needs to refer to it.\n102\t- Check that supplied rule references exist and identifiers are unambiguous.\n103\t\n104\tMissing summaries and ambiguous rule references may produce draft hints. Hints\n105\thelp a reviewer; they do not withhold approval. Existing approved text need not\n106\tbe rewritten to adopt these checklists.\n107\t\n108\t## architecture\n109\t\n110\t### Checklist\n111\t\n112\t- `addresses` names only architecturally significant requirements; `conforms_to` names the specifications it must respect.\n113\t- Components, responsibilities, dependency direction, and trust boundaries are each stated.\n114\t- The decision assessment is complete: `adr_required` with triggers, or `no_significant_decision` with a rationale.\n115\t- For material added complexity, what simpler approach was considered, including existing capabilities? Explain why it is insufficient and the added cost across development, testing, operation and change.\n116\t\n117\t## adr\n118\t\n119\t### Checklist\n120\t\n121\t- Context, drivers, at least two considered options with consequences, the decision, and its consequences.\n122\t- The chosen option is stated as a decision, not a preference.\n123\t- If the chosen option adds material complexity, why is the simpler option insufficient for the agreed constraints, and is the added cost justified?\n124\t- Every `decides` target architecture is an active artifact.\n125\t\n126\t## verification\n127\t\n128\t### Checklist\n129\t\n130\t- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\n131\t- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\n132\t- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\n133\t- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Cover distinct relevant outcomes, reuse applicable evidence, and avoid mirroring every implementation branch or combination.\n134\t- Pass criteria name the platforms and the evaluator.\n135\t\n136\t## work_order\n137\t\n138\t### Checklist\n139\t\n140\t- `[execution_scope].paths` is exact: files or component-prefix directories, nothing wider.\n141\t- `[assurance]` is classified and its rationale names what later decisions depend on.\n142\t- In scope and out of scope are both stated; the decision envelope says what the implementer may and may not decide.\n143\t- Stop conditions and the completion report format are stated.\n144\t- Is every planned change needed for the agreed outcome? Include removal or adjustment of obsolete behavior, tests and instructions within the approved scope.\n145\t- Inspect supporting paths before approval and assess the supplied plan through [Draft work orders](harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Keep reasons in Expected change surface; do not add a separate planning artifact.\n146\t\n147\t## Review of implemented changes\n148\t\n149\t- Does the result satisfy the necessary contract, and did implementation reveal unnecessary specified behavior? Resolve an excessive approved obligation through the existing amendment process before changing its behavior.\n150\t- Is each material addition needed? Could a direct solution or existing capability meet the same constraints with less total work to understand, test, operate and change?\n151\t- Does the normal design or review evidence explain significant complexity and its cost? Remove unused generality or indirection that has no agreed purpose.\n152\t- Do tests and CI establish the accepted behavior and relevant quality properties? Remove obsolete checks together with the obligations they no longer serve.\n153\t\n154\tRetain material findings and their resolution with the ordinary review evidence.\n155\tApplying these questions is part of that review, not a second review or receipt.\n156\t\n157\t## release_contract\n158\t\n159\t### Checklist\n160\t\n161\t- `gates` names every work order the release may include.\n162\t- Version, rollback conditions, and evidence expectations are explicit.\n163\t\n164\t## operating_contract\n165\t\n166\t### Checklist\n167\t\n168\t- `assures` names every requirement it claims continuing assurance for.\n169\t- Observability, support, and operating obligations are measurable.\n170\t\n171\t## decision\n172\t\n173\tA pending question becomes a `decision` (`DEC-`) when it blocks a transition\n174\tof another artifact, concerns more than one artifact, or must survive the\n175\tapproval of the artifact that raised it. Below that threshold the actor asks\n176\tand the answer stays in the transition's `reason`.\n177\t\n178\t### Checklist\n179\t\n180\t- `kind` is `question` (an ambiguity met while authoring or planning) or `deviation` (an implementation cannot meet one rule of one specification). *(mechanical)*\n181\t- One `question`, `raised_by`, at least two `[[options]]` with `id` and `label`, and a `recommendation` naming one option. *(mechanical)*\n182\t- A deviation also names `against = \"SPEC-xxx#PREFIX-AREA-NNN\"`, a rule identifier the specification defines *(mechanical: E-DCM-005)*, and the `observed` fact; its options are drawn from `amend`, `supersede`, `accept`, `stop` and include `stop`. *(mechanical)*\n183\t- `concerns` names every artifact the question is about; `blocks` names the artifacts that cannot change state while it is `open`, each also in `concerns`. *(mechanical)*\n184\t- The `[disposition]` table is written by `harnessctl decide`; a hand-written one is `E-DCM-003`. A deferral needs a scope and a revisit trigger; accepting a deviation needs a revisit trigger. *(mechanical)*\n185\t- `decided` and `withdrawn` decisions are never deleted or rewritten.\n186\t- The definition templates carry no `Open decisions` section: a definition's pending decisions are the `DEC-` artifacts that name it in `blocks`, and the approval gate reads them from the graph. A legacy section, where one remains, reads `None` or lists `DEC-` identifiers; prose there is `E-DCM-004`.\n187\t\n188\t## risk\n189\t\n190\t### Checklist\n191\t\n192\t- Record a description, an owner and a next action.\n193\t- Use `harnessctl raise-risk --description TEXT --action TEXT --owner ROLE` with a domain and title.\n194\t- Stage, category, threatened artifacts and scoring are optional. If scoring is useful, supply likelihood and impact together (1–5); the command computes their product.\n195\t- Request `--with-decision` only when the owner needs a decision that blocks named artifacts. A risk by itself does not block work.\n196\t- Keep earlier scores, decisions and dispositions as recorded; no migration is required.\n197\t\n198\t\n199\t## verification_record and release_record\n200\t\n201\tPrepared by `capture-verification` and `prepare-release`; they are not\n202\tauthored by hand. This policy does not apply to them.\n203\t"
}

{
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_01GCjEXKLWCt4M9RSSYdbxbu",
  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "1\t# Private lifecycle rehearsal wire contract\n2\t\n3\tThe v2 endpoint accepts only `lifecycle-v2.json`. Every request and result selects\n4\ttest data. Git remains authoritative for real work. Existing v1 routes and the\n5\tseven read-only MCP tools keep their contract; they cannot dispatch v2 actions.\n6\tIn an explicitly configured test project their results use\n7\t`se-harness-graph-read/v2`, with a required test-copy label. Ordinary Phase 2\n8\tprojects retain their v1 read result shapes. Revision byte hashing is unchanged;\n9\ttest revisions add explicit test-copy provenance, without rewriting v1 fixtures.\n10\t\n11\t`inspect` accepts check, preflight and validate. `preview` runs a mutation in a\n12\tdiscarded projection and returns its input digest and complete proposed file\n13\teffects. `apply` requires that digest, reruns the released checks, and commits\n14\tthe complete result once. A preview never changes graph state. Actor strings\n15\tare synthetic test inputs, not authenticated human consent.\n16\t\n17\tThe digest binds project/context versions, all selected revision IDs, retained\n18\tsnapshot, exact command/file inputs, principal and released evaluator identity.\n19\tMode and operation key are excluded from the preview binding, but included in\n20\tthe accepted request digest used for principal-scoped retry lookup.\n21\t\n22\t## Permitted file effects\n23\t\n24\t| Action | Permitted effects in the disposable projection |\n25\t| --- | --- |\n26\t| check / preflight / validate | No file writes |\n27\t| transition | Only selected newly authored artifacts and evaluator sidecars declared by their records |\n28\t| decide | Selected new decision and its explicitly linked rehearsal risk |\n29\t| raise-risk | New risk and optional paired decision at released domain paths |\n30\t| handoff | Declared bounded file inputs within the selected work order's scope; released handoff packet and evaluator evidence |\n31\t| capture-verification | New VREC and its released evaluator sidecar |\n32\t| prepare-release | New RLS and its released evaluator sidecar |\n33\t\n34\tHandoff first uses the released procedure's fixed `evidence` preparation command,\n35\tthen `check --checkpoint handoff --from-git`. It exposes no arbitrary evidence\n36\tcommand. File inputs cannot alter formal records, harness selection/instructions,\n37\tGit configuration, hooks, or executables used by the service. The service never\n38\truns source tests. The local qualification runner supplies observed test evidence.\n39\t\n40\tThe adapter enumerates every regular file before and after the operation. It\n41\trejects deletions, links, unexpected paths, missing sidecars, imported-record\n42\tchanges and evaluator failure before opening an acceptance transaction.\n43\t\n44\t## Snapshots and Git\n45\t\n46\tEach accepted operation retains an immutable complete snapshot: exact file bytes,\n47\tself-contained Git history and its source/test-candidate identities. Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n85\t\n86\t```cypher\n87\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n88\t```\n89\t\n90\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n91\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n92\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n93\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n94\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n95\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n96\t"
}

Do not remove an unresolved\\nquestion merely to make validation pass.\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected package has an explicit list of review findings\\nand approval blockers. No approval is inferred from these checks.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers) resolves the blockers. [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) presents the package for a\\nhuman decision.\\n\\n### Resolve approval blockers\\n\\n**Inputs:** The findings from [Assess the proposed artifact package](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#assess-the-proposed-artifact-package) and the affected draft artifacts or\\npending decisions (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Corrected drafts within the proposed scope. Where an\\n  actual human decision is supplied, the selected decision (DEC) receives its\\n  disposition. A paired risk (RISK) may change through that same operation.\\n- **Transient working material:** The resolution of each finding and any\\n  remaining dependency requiring an amendment or another human decision.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Correct incomplete or inconsistent draft content within the requested scope.\\n2. Preserve unresolved blockers.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact DEC-ID --json\\nharnessctl decide REPO --artifact DEC-ID --option OPTION-ID --decision ACTOR --reason \\\"Exact decision reason\\\" --json\\n```\\n\\nUse a declared option ID. Add `--revisit \\\"Trigger\\\"` for acceptance of a\\ndeviation. A risk-mitigation option also requires `--mitigated-by WO-ID` for\\neach selected mitigating work order (WO). For a risk-avoidance option, use\\n`--avoided-by ARTIFACT-ID` when naming a separate architecture decision (ADR)\\nor decision (DEC). Review the returned operation before applying it.\\n\\nACTOR is the actual human who made the decision. If that human acts under an\\nexisting owner label, first resolve their authority under\\n[Decision rights](AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights), then add\\n`--authority-owner OWNER` to both preview and apply. For example, a human\\nnamed `mmzen` acting under `engineering-owner` uses\\n`--decision mmzen --authority-owner engineering-owner`. Do not put the owner\\nlabel in place of the human. Neither string authenticates the decision.\\nOWNER must be non-blank printable text of at most 128 characters and match\\nan eligible owner exactly. An unrelated label refuses without writing.\\n\\nFor an explicitly authorized deferral, use this preview instead:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl decide REPO --artifact DEC-ID --defer --scope ARTIFACT-ID:FROM-TO --revisit \\\"Trigger\\\" --decision ACTOR --reason \\\"Exact decision reason\\\" --json\\n```\\n\\nRepeat `--scope` for each permitted transition. For an explicitly authorized\\nwithdrawal, replace the option or deferral arguments with `--withdraw`.\\nApply a passing preview by repeating the exact command with `--apply`.\\nThen inspect the decision (DEC) again and rerun validation:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact DEC-ID --json\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nIf proposing a departure from a `SHOULD` rule, record the rule ID, reason,\\nimpact, accountable human or agent, and compensating evidence. Present these\\nwith the affected review finding. Recording the departure does not authorize\\nan action or waive a required check.\\n\\n**Completion:** Each blocker is resolved or assigned an explicit outstanding\\ndecision. A deferral clears only its recorded transition scope.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) receives the corrected package. No lifecycle state\\n  changes in this step.\\n- **Transient working material:** The human's approval, rejection, or request\\n  for revision, bound to exact artifact IDs, reviewed contents, and authority.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Present one concise request for the coherent package using the decision\\n   card below. Identify the selected artifacts and proposed state for each.\\n2. Present the scope, verification obligations, proposed assurance classification,\\n   its rationale, and unresolved findings.\\n3. Identify the human accountable for each requested decision.\\n4. Retain a SHA-256 digest of each reviewed definition's complete file bytes.\\n5. Obtain an explicit approval, rejection, or request for revision. Work-order\\n   approval must include confirmation of the assurance classification. Record that\\n   confirmation in the work order's `[assurance]` fields before the approval\\n   preview. A rejection needs a reason. Reuse an existing decision while its\\n   reviewed inputs match.\\n6. If revision is requested, return the affected drafts to [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers). Do not\\n   interpret a request for revision as a terminal rejection.\\n\\n**Harness command:** None. A command result cannot supply the human decision.\\n\\n**Decision card:**\\n\\n- **Approval requested:** Name the decision and useful outcome in ordinary\\n  language. Use \\\"Approve implementation\\\" for local implementation authority.\\n  For PR-based work, use \\\"Approve implementation and review publication\\\" and\\n  describe the bounded grant from [Review publication authority](AUTHORITY.md#review-publication-authority).\\n  Verification acceptance and merge remain separate decisions.\\n- **Why:** State the problem this change solves.\\n- **What changes:** Summarize the bounded changes, including supporting work.\\n- **What success looks like:** State the observable result and required checks.\\n- **Important limit:** State material uncertainty or an unresolved boundary,\\n  when one exists. Do not hide it behind a passing coverage report.\\n- **Your approval permits:** State the exact permitted actions and any\\n  separate acceptance or external-action decision relevant to this request.\\n- **Review details:** Link the exact definitions, work orders and verification\\n  contracts, their proposed states and their reviewed revisions.\\n\\nOffer \\\"Approve implementation\\\" and \\\"Request changes\\\" when implementation is\\nthe decision. When the described grant includes review publication, offer\\n\\\"Approve implementation and review publication\\\" instead. Name the repository,\\nsource branch, target branch, draft PR and later verification-decision push\\nin \\\"Your approval permits.\\\" The human need not type artifact IDs or CLI commands. Bind the\\nanswer to the displayed package and permission. Reuse an actual prior decision\\nwhile its reviewed inputs still match; do not ask for the same authority again.\\n\\n**Completion:** Each selected artifact has an explicit human decision or\\nremains pending. Pending artifacts are not included in an approval transaction.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions) applies only the transitions named by these decisions.\\n\\n### Apply the selected transitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact artifact IDs, target states, accountable human identities,\\nreviewed file digests, decisions from [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision), and clarification answers from\\n[Define the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#procedure) that must be retained in the transition reason.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only explicitly selected definitions and work orders\\n  (WO) receive the applied state and lifecycle-history entries.\\n- **Transient working material:** The preview and apply results, including\\n  any refused transition.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n### Confirm the authorization result\\n\\n**Inputs:** The applied transition result and selected artifact files.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None changed by readback.\\n- **Transient working material:** The authorization handoff, stating actual\\n  states, execution readiness, remaining blockers, and the evaluator's next step.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Read the selected artifact states and new lifecycle-history entries.\\n2. Inspect each selected work order (WO) with `check`.\\n3. For an approved work order (WO), run start preflight to assess readiness.\\n4. Report the approved scope and any remaining start blockers. Report rejected\\n   or pending work separately.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\n```\\n\\nUse the preflight command only for work proposed to start. An approved\\nwork order (WO) remains `approved` until the start transition is applied.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Execute the work](EXECUTE_WORK.md#procedure) uses the recorded approval. Inspect the selected work order (WO).\\n2. Read the formal artifacts in `context.reading_manifest`. For instructions,\\n   read the selected procedure's entry conditions, current step, and prerequisites\\n   whose conditions apply. Reuse current required material still in context;\\n   reread it when changed or lost. After compaction, obtain a fresh `check`\\n   result before continuing. This does not remove any required formal input.\\n3. Read the approved behavior, permitted paths, decision limits, stop\\n   conditions, verification contracts (VER), and required repository checks.\\n4. Identify the complete change baseline required by the selected procedure.\\n   Retain its full commit ID as `BASE`. Do not choose a newer base merely to\\n   exclude earlier changes from the checks.\\n5. Compare the planned work with the recorded approval. Return changed scope\\n   to definition and authorization before executing it.\\n\\n**Harness command:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The executor has an explicit approved scope and the inputs\\nrequired by the selected procedure. Missing baseline or scope information\\nis reported before dependent work.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Start the selected work](EXECUTE_WORK.md#start-the-selected-work), [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope), [Retain the implementation evidence](EXECUTE_WORK.md#retain-the-implementation-evidence), [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) use this scope, baseline, and verification plan.\\n\\n### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n### Implement the approved scope\\n\\n**Inputs:** The work order (WO) in `in_progress`, approved definitions,\\npermitted paths, and baseline from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Repository files:** The code, tests, documentation, or configuration\\n  changes authorized by the work order (WO).\\n- **Formal artifacts:** No lifecycle transition in this step. Artifact content\\n  changes require the authority and procedure applicable to those records.\\n- **Transient working material:** Implementation notes and unresolved findings.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the files and behavior to be changed.\\n2. Compare the complete actual and planned path set with the approved scope.\\n3. Run the scope check below with every actual and planned path.\\n4. Make the changes allowed by the work order (WO).\\n5. Run local checks as needed to guide the implementation. Keep failures\\n   available for the evidence record.\\n6. Review the diff against the accepted definitions. Apply\\n   [Review of implemented changes](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#review-of-implemented-changes).\\n7. Resolve in-scope defects. Stop affected work when a finding requires changed\\n   requirements, paths, or authority.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nRepeat `--changed-path PATH` for the complete set, including planned new files:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint scope --changed-path PATH --changes-complete --json\\n```\\n\\nThis declaration is checked evidence, not independent proof of completeness.\\nWhen the selected procedure requires a `pre-action` check, use its returned\\ncommand with the complete change-set inputs. For Git-derived actual changes:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint pre-action --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nUse the repository's editing, build, and test tools for implementation.\\n`harnessctl` does not implement the change.\\n\\n**Completion:** The approved behavior is implemented within scope, or the\\nremaining work and blocker are explicit. No completion state is inferred yet.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Retain the implementation evidence](EXECUTE_WORK.md#retain-the-implementation-evidence) checks the result and retains its evidence.\\n\\n### Retain the implementation evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented changes, verification contracts (VER), required\\nrepository checks, and work-order evidence locations.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** Actual check results, review findings, their\\n  resolution, and Git-derived handoff evidence at authorized evidence paths.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Evidence references may be added where the work order\\n  (WO) requires them. No lifecycle state changes here.\\n- **Transient working material:** A summary of remaining evidence gaps.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run every required check using its specified inputs and environment.\\n2. Retain the command arguments, working directory, runtime identity, exit\\n   status, output, and relevant file paths or digests.\\n3. Preserve failed results alongside later corrections and successful reruns.\\n4. Retain the material diff-review findings and their resolution.\\n5. Confirm that all required evidence files exist at the locations named by\\n   the work order (WO) or verification contract (VER).\\n6. Run review preflight.\\n7. Run the Git-derived handoff check with the trusted `BASE` from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n8. Inspect its written evidence and reported findings. Correct in-scope gaps\\n   and rerun affected checks.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase review --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nFor ordinary files listed in the work order\\u2019s `evidence_paths`, confirm that\\neach file exists inside the repository and contains the required evidence.\\nThese files do not need a machine header. Use the supported evidence command\\nfor generated packets; do not edit their evaluator-owned binding fields.\\n\\nThe Git-derived handoff command writes retained evidence. Confirm its output\\npaths are covered by the approved preparation scope. If the evaluator calls\\nfor creating or rebinding the handoff evidence packet, use:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl evidence REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --json\\n```\\n\\nThis command also writes a file. It does not run tests or make an unsupported\\nclaim true. Rerun the required handoff check after correcting the evidence gap.\\n\\n**Completion:** Required implementation checks pass and the actual evidence\\nis retained, or the exact missing or failed checks remain visible.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) requires passing handoff results. Verification later\\nassesses these retained files; they are not disposable planning notes.\\n\\n### Record implementation completion\\n\\n**Inputs:** Completed work, passing handoff checks, retained evidence, and\\nthe unchanged approved execution scope.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `implemented`\\n  with the actual executor recorded in its lifecycle history.\\n- **Transient working material:** The completion report and current next step.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the work and required evidence are complete.\\n2. Preview the completion transition using the executor's own identity.\\n3. Apply the same transition when the preview passes.\\n4. Inspect the resulting work order (WO).\\n5. Report the completed behavior, checks, limitations, and evaluator's next\\n   action. Preserve failures if completion was refused.\\n\\nUse the passing handoff result from the preceding step. A passing completion\\npreview does not replace the handoff check. If the implementation, governing\\nartifacts, or required evidence changed after handoff, rerun the affected\\nchecks before applying completion. If `harnessctl` refuses the retained\\nhandoff evidence, follow its reported corrective step.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=implemented --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=implemented --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The work order (WO) is `implemented`, or its completion is\\nblocked. Completion does not verify, release, or deliver the result.\\n\\n**Later use:** Work requiring commit-bound verification proceeds to\\n[Verify the outcome](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#establish-the-verification-scope).\\nAfter preparation, PR-based work first follows [Publish the review package](PULL_REQUEST.md#publish-the-review-package).\\nThen use the [verification request](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#obtain-the-humans-verification-decision)\\nwhen the ready record is eligible and its PR is confirmed. Local-only work\\nuses that verification request without a PR. The completion report introduces no\\nadditional human approval. An implemented work order (WO) classified `not_required`\\nneeds no new verification record (VREC); follow its actual next action and\\nany separately authorized delivery instruction.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"eb6c0ea9807f841a877ca23432cd9559683395700b1349edc4ea5ad652193e27\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/VERIFY_OUTCOME.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-lifecycle-native26-20261010\\\\claude-212\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\VERIFY_OUTCOME.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"84686ea50e0e220aadc024145b88e05d83a0eab01be361ab50e12716a8cf950a\", \"bytes\": 18538, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"procedure\", \"title\": \"Procedure\", \"anchor\": \"procedure\", \"start_line\": 30, \"end_line\": 351, \"content\": \"## Procedure\\n\\n### Establish the verification scope\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented work orders (WO), candidate commit, verification\\ncontracts (VER), and any existing verification records (VREC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None changed.\\n- **Transient working material:** The selected work-order (WO) set, full\\n  candidate commit ID, contract IDs, evidence paths, and applicable procedure.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the selected work order (WO) or existing verification record (VREC).\\n2. Read its governing definitions, verification contracts (VER), and evidence.\\n3. Confirm the exact set of work orders (WO) being assessed.\\n4. Confirm the complete set of verification contracts (VER) declared by them.\\n5. Identify the full candidate commit and the retained evidence files.\\n6. Check whether the selected procedure requires a new verification record\\n   (VREC), an existing-record decision, or no new record.\\n7. If no new record is required, report that result and follow the returned\\n   next step. Do not claim a verification decision that has not occurred.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nUse the command matching the selected record:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact VREC-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The exact candidate and verification scope are selected, and\\nthe need for a new record is known.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Assess the verification evidence](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#assess-the-verification-evidence), [Prepare the verification record](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#prepare-the-verification-record), [Obtain the human's verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#obtain-the-humans-verification-decision), [Record the verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#record-the-verification-decision) use these exact inputs. Changed candidate or evidence\\ninputs require reassessment before reusing a decision.\\n\\n### Assess the verification evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected candidate, accepted definitions, verification\\ncontracts (VER), and retained implementation evidence.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** A requirement-by-requirement assessment with\\n  actual check results and unresolved gaps at the specified evidence locations.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** No verification decision or lifecycle transition.\\n- **Transient working material:** Review notes not required as retained evidence.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Compare the candidate's observed behavior with each acceptance criterion.\\n2. Map every required check to its actual evidence file and pass condition.\\n3. Confirm that the evidence applies to the selected candidate and environment.\\n4. Run missing checks using the verification contract's (VER) commands or\\n   manual procedure. Retain their actual results.\\n5. Record each criterion as passed, failed, or not assessed, with its reason.\\n6. Retain material findings and their resolution with the verification evidence.\\n7. For a defect requiring implementation changes, identify a work order that\\n   covers the correction and is eligible for execution. Follow the recovery\\n   rules under \\u201cIf a procedure is blocked.\\u201d A completed work order does not\\n   become executable again because verification found a defect.\\n8. After the authorized correction, establish the new candidate identity and\\n   reassess the affected criteria against evidence for that candidate.\\n\\n**Harness command:** None for the substantive assessment. Use the check\\ncommands and manual assessments defined in the verification contracts (VER).\\nHarness validation alone does not prove the required behavior.\\n\\n**Completion:** Every required criterion has evidence or a visible gap.\\nFailures and missing checks are not reported as passes.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Prepare the verification record](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#prepare-the-verification-record) binds the retained evidence to a verification record\\n(VREC). [Obtain the human's verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#obtain-the-humans-verification-decision) uses it for the human's assessment.\\n\\n### Prepare the verification record\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact candidate, selected work orders (WO), their verification\\ncontracts (VER), retained evidence, and existing preparation authority.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** One new verification record (VREC) in `ready` when\\n  capture is required and succeeds. Related work-order (WO) states do not change.\\n- **Retained files:** The generated evaluator evidence and candidate snapshot\\n  or export required by the capture procedure.\\n- **Transient working material:** The capture result and inspected output paths.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If a suitable verification record (VREC) already exists, inspect it and\\n   continue to [Obtain the human's verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#obtain-the-humans-verification-decision). Do not duplicate an interrupted successful capture.\\n2. Confirm preparation authority under every selected work order's (WO)\\n   approval. Reuse it while the scope and inputs match.\\n3. Select an unused `VREC-ID` after checking the repository's local Git refs.\\n4. Confirm the domain and actual record and evidence destinations. Keep all\\n   generated writes within the authorized preparation scope.\\n5. Establish a clean committed candidate using authorized local Git actions.\\n   Retain its full commit ID. Do not discard unrelated changes to obtain it.\\n6. Run the capture command with each selected work order (WO), every declared\\n   verification contract (VER), and each retained evidence file.\\n7. Read the generated record and evaluator evidence. Confirm the candidate\\n   commit, selected IDs, evidence digests, and `ready` state.\\n8. Retain generated records and evidence in a later authorized governance\\n   commit. Preserve the earlier candidate commit that the record assesses.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl capture-verification REPO --id VREC-ID --work-order WO-ID --verification VER-ID --evidence EVIDENCE-PATH --owner ACTOR --domain DOMAIN --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact VREC-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nUse the actual preparation actor for `--owner`. Preparation does not record\\na future verification or release decision. Preserve the generated record and\\nits bound evidence without changing the recorded evidence bytes.\\n\\nRepeat `--work-order`, `--verification`, and `--evidence` for every selected\\ninput. `EVIDENCE-PATH` is a repository-relative file, not a directory or an\\nindex whose links will be followed automatically. Capture writes immediately\\nafter its checks; it has no `--dry-run` or `--apply` option.\\n\\nIf an unchanged rebase leaves an earlier ready record bound to the old\\ncandidate, use [Refresh verification after an unchanged rebase](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#refresh-verification-after-an-unchanged-rebase).\\nA verified source or changed relevant inputs cannot use that shortcut.\\n\\nIf preserving local edits requires the supported committed-candidate route,\\nuse `--candidate-commit COMMIT` with `--test-command EXECUTABLE ARGUMENTS`.\\nPut `--test-command` last. That route tests the named commit in a temporary\\ncheckout; use its actual results and returned record, not results from the\\nuncommitted edits.\\n\\n**Completion:** A ready verification record (VREC) binds the selected candidate\\nand evidence, or capture reports a specific refusal. Preparation is not verification.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Obtain the human's verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#obtain-the-humans-verification-decision) presents the exact record and evidence for the human decision.\\n\\n### Obtain the human's verification decision\\n\\nFor work delivered through a PR, first complete\\n[Publish the review package](PULL_REQUEST.md#publish-the-review-package).\\nDo not request verification before the exact remote review package is confirmed.\\nUse the evaluator's declared review-publication alternative, not its later\\nintegration path. Local-only work does not acquire a PR requirement.\\n\\n**Inputs:** The ready verification record (VREC), candidate commit, evidence\\ndigests, assessment results, and accountable human's verification authority.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None changed by the discussion or read-only gate check.\\n- **Transient working material:** The human's exact verification, rejection,\\n  or supersession decision and reason where required.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Prepare the concise verification request below for the exact record,\\n   candidate, evidence, and unresolved findings.\\n2. For verification or rejection, evaluate the proposed target with the\\n   command below. Supersession gates are evaluated with the selected successor\\n   in [Record the verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#record-the-verification-decision)'s transition preview.\\n3. Obtain the accountable human's decision on that exact verification record\\n   (VREC). Preserve the required independence from implementation.\\n4. Reuse a previous decision only while its record, candidate, evidence digests,\\n   authority, and current gates still match.\\n5. For rejection, retain the reason. For supersession, identify the exact\\n   eligible successor verification record (VREC).\\n\\n**Harness command:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact VREC-ID --checkpoint transition --target TARGET --json\\n```\\n\\nUse `verified` or `rejected` for `TARGET`. The evaluator determines whether\\nthat edge is legal. Supersession needs the successor input accepted by\\n`transition --reason`, which this check command does not accept.\\nA passed check is not the human decision.\\n\\n**Verification request:**\\n\\n- **Verification requested - [outcome]:** Name the useful result being assessed.\\n- **Delivered:** Describe the behavior delivered against the agreed outcome.\\n- **Evidence:** Summarize the requirement results and meaningful checks. Link\\n  the requirement-by-requirement assessment and retained evidence. Test counts\\n  support the explanation; they do not replace it.\\n- **Limits:** State material failures, skipped or unavailable checks, unassessed\\n  criteria, and residual uncertainty. Say that no material gaps are known only\\n  when the evidence supports it.\\n- **Your decision:** Explain that \\\"Verify result\\\" accepts this exact candidate\\n  against the agreed requirements. Merge and release remain separate decisions.\\n- **Review details:** For PR-based work, show the confirmed PR URL prominently.\\n  Identify the exact VREC and full candidate commit. Link its governing work\\n  orders, verification contracts, assessment, and evidence at the remote review\\n  commit so the owner can read them without access to the workstation.\\n  Local-only work uses its available review locations.\\n\\nLead with ordinary language and keep the request proportionate to the work.\\nDetails may be linked, but material gaps and the decision's scope must be\\nvisible in the request. Keep passed, failed, and not-assessed results distinct.\\nAn accepted risk needs its recorded decision reference; otherwise it remains\\nunresolved. Do not silently bundle risk acceptance or waive required checks.\\n\\nOffer \\\"Verify result\\\" and \\\"Request corrections\\\" when verification is eligible.\\nIf verification is blocked, report the blocker and evaluator's next action\\ninstead of offering acceptance.\\nThe human need not type IDs or commands. Bind an unambiguous \\\"I verify\\\" or\\n\\\"Verify result\\\" to the displayed record, candidate, and evidence before applying\\nthe decision. Clarify an ambiguous reply. Reassess changed inputs under action 4;\\nsilence or implementation approval does not supply verification acceptance.\\n\\n\\\"Request corrections\\\" leaves the decision pending; it is not a terminal\\nrejection. Follow [Assess the verification evidence](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#assess-the-verification-evidence)\\nto identify the correction and eligible execution authority. A completed work\\norder does not become executable again. An explicit rejection or supersession\\nstill follows action 5 and [Record the verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#record-the-verification-decision).\\n\\n**Completion:** The exact verification decision is known, or the record remains\\npending. No implementation actor infers acceptance from their own passing tests.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record the verification decision](VERIFY_OUTCOME.md#record-the-verification-decision) records only the selected decision.\\n\\n### Record the verification decision\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact human decision, ready verification record (VREC), and\\nunchanged candidate and evidence inputs.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only the selected verification record (VREC) receives\\n  its permitted state and lifecycle-history entry.\\n- **Retained files:** The updated record is retained through authorized Git\\n  actions. Historical candidate and evidence facts are preserved.\\n- **Transient working material:** The observed decision result and next action.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Recheck that the selected inputs still match the human decision.\\n2. Preview the exact transition.\\n3. Apply the same command when its gates pass and effects match the decision.\\n4. Inspect the verification record (VREC) and resulting workflow context.\\n5. Report its actual state, any blocker, and the evaluator's next action.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set VREC-ID=TARGET --decision VREC-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set VREC-ID=TARGET --decision VREC-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact VREC-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nFor supersession, select a successor verification record that is already\\n`verified` or `released` and preserves the old record\\u2019s work-order coverage.\\nUse the transition preview to assess that selection.\\n\\nFor `rejected`, add `--reason \\\"VREC-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to preview and apply.\\nFor `superseded`, add `--reason \\\"VREC-ID=SUCCESSOR-VREC-ID\\\"` to both. Preserve\\nthe old record; do not rewrite its candidate or evidence to match a new result.\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected verification record (VREC) has the recorded\\ndecision. Referenced work orders (WO) and release records (RLS) remain unchanged.\\n\\n**Later use:** For PR-based work, [Publish the verification decision](PULL_REQUEST.md#publish-the-verification-decision)\\nas the final commit under the existing grant. Eligible verified coverage\\nsupports the selected delivery path.\\nA rejection or supersession follows the evaluator's remediation or selection\\nprocedure; it does not authorize new implementation scope.\\n\\n### Refresh verification after an unchanged rebase\\n\\n**Inputs:** An existing verification record in `ready`, a new clean committed\\ncandidate, the unchanged work/contract/evidence selection, and preparation\\nauthority under each selected work order's approval.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifact:** One new verification record in `ready`, with\\n  `refreshed_from` naming the original record, if the comparison passes.\\n- **Retained evidence:** New evaluator provenance for the new record. The\\n  original record and its evidence remain intact.\\n- **Transient working material:** The selected IDs, comparison result, and\\n  any refusal. No new test result is claimed.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Read the original record and confirm it is `ready`. A verified record is\\n   not a valid refresh source.\\n2. Select the new candidate commit. Confirm the worktree is clean.\\n3. Select an unused new VREC ID after checking the local Git refs, as in the\\n   verification-record preparation procedure.\\n4. Read the original work-order, verification-contract, and evidence selections.\\n   The command reuses them; do not substitute a different selection.\\n5. Run the refresh command below using the actual preparation actor.\\n6. Inspect the result. The evaluator compares relevant Git tree entries,\\n   including governing files, execution scope, harness inputs, and evidence.\\n7. If relevant inputs changed, return to fresh tests and verification capture.\\n   Do not edit evidence or reduce scope to force the comparison to pass.\\n8. Read the new record and confirm its candidate, selections, provenance, and\\n   `refreshed_from`. Confirm the original record remains unchanged.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact OLD-VREC-ID --json\\nharnessctl refresh-verification REPO --from OLD-VREC-ID --id NEW-VREC-ID --owner ACTOR --domain DOMAIN --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact NEW-VREC-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nRefresh writes after its checks. It has no `--dry-run` or `--apply` option.\\nIt reruns no tests and verifies neither record. Equality of relevant Git\\nentries is assessed by the evaluator, not inferred from the word \\u201crebase.\\u201d\\n\\n**Completion:** A new ready record binds the new candidate, or a refusal names\\nthe changed or invalid inputs. The original record's state is unchanged.\\n\\n**Later use:** Return to the human verification decision in \\u201cVerify the outcome.\\u201d\\nIf the original ready record should be superseded, use its own explicitly\\nselected supersession procedure after an eligible successor exists.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"862dc431d7ab02aaf85b654f44dc7da53b2f3f1e68fddf86fa421d45d9ffbdae\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/RELEASE.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-lifecycle-native26-20261010\\\\claude-212\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\RELEASE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"ce700f8f764c4425978f070d978d48eacafb927601f45e298c765f46dae90f7f\", \"bytes\": 7849, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"procedure\", \"title\": \"Procedure\", \"anchor\": \"procedure\", \"start_line\": 11, \"end_line\": 159, \"content\": \"## Procedure\\n\\n### Prepare a release record\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected path, existing preparation authority, exact candidate,\\nand the release contract (REL) and eligible verified coverage required by that\\npath. Release-record preparation requires both.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Release path:** One release record (RLS) in `ready` and its generated\\n  evaluator evidence, when preparation is required and succeeds.\\n- **Retained files:** Required build or delivery evidence at the authorized\\n  locations. Builds occur only when separately covered by the selected scope.\\n- **Transient working material:** Preparation notes, proposed external content,\\n  and inspected write destinations.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm authority for the exact preparation operation. Obtain a missing\\n   human decision before writing; reuse matching authority already supplied.\\n2. Inspect existing records and outputs before creating replacements.\\n3. For release, select an active release contract (REL), version, domain,\\n   and unused release record (RLS) ID. Check local Git refs and existing\\n   version reservations before choosing the ID and version.\\n4. Select the final eligible verification record (VREC) for the candidate.\\n   Its work-order (WO) set must exactly cover the work being released.\\n5. Confirm all intended output paths and the clean working-tree condition\\n   required by release preparation.\\n6. Run the release preparation command. Inspect its written record, evidence,\\n   candidate identity, and `ready` state.\\n7. Retain the new release record (RLS) and evidence in an authorized governance\\n   commit after the candidate they describe.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor release preparation:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl prepare-release REPO --id RLS-ID --release-contract REL-ID --verification-record VREC-ID --work-order WO-ID --version VERSION --owner ACTOR --domain DOMAIN --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact RLS-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nUse the actual preparation actor for `--owner`. Preparation does not record\\na future verification or release decision. Preserve the generated record and\\nits bound evidence without changing the recorded evidence bytes.\\n\\nRepeat `--work-order` for every released work order (WO). This evaluator requires one final verification record (VREC) covering the complete\\nrelease. Earlier records remain supporting evidence. If that coverage is\\nmissing, return to verification instead of inventing or relabelling it.\\n\\nAdd `--tag TAG` only for an explicitly selected tag value; this records the\\nvalue and does not create a Git tag. `prepare-release` writes after its checks;\\nit has no `--dry-run` or `--apply` option.\\n\\n**Completion:** The required delivery inputs are prepared, or a precise\\npreparation blocker is known. Preparation performs no merge, publication,\\ndeployment, or release decision.\\n\\n**Later use:** A ready release record (RLS) proceeds to [Obtain the release decision, when required](RELEASE.md#obtain-the-release-decision-when-required). \\n\\n### Obtain the release decision, when required\\n\\n**Inputs:** The ready release record (RLS), release contract (REL), candidate,\\nverified coverage, version, and accountable human's release authority.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None changed by review or the gate check.\\n- **Transient working material:** The human's exact release or rejection\\n  decision, including the reason for rejection.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Present the candidate, included work, verification coverage, version,\\n   constraints, rollback conditions, and required release evidence.\\n   If the contract and delivery plan select complete release delivery, follow\\n   [Complete-release authority](AUTHORITY.md#complete-release-authority). Finish\\n   required deliverable preparation and human verification before this request.\\n   Show one concise request: \\\"Approve the complete release\\\", its versions,\\n   delivery actions, verification summary, limitations and a link to the exact\\n   plan. Include payload digests, destinations, marker targets and any bounded\\n   release-only integration in that plan.\\n2. Run the transition gate check for the intended target state.\\n3. Obtain the accountable human's decision on the selected release record\\n   (RLS). Reuse a matching decision already supplied.\\n   For complete delivery, the same response explicitly covers its listed external\\n   actions. Retain the actual human, decision reference and reviewed plan digest.\\n   Do not rewrite the plan when public observations arrive; retain them separately.\\n4. Retain a rejection reason when applicable.\\n\\n**Harness command:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact RLS-ID --checkpoint transition --target TARGET --json\\n```\\n\\nUse `released` or `rejected` for `TARGET`, according to the proposed decision.\\nThe evaluator determines legality. A passed check does not supply approval.\\n\\n**Completion:** The release decision is explicit, or remains pending.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record the release decision, when required](RELEASE.md#record-the-release-decision-when-required) records that decision. Execute listed delivery actions\\nunder the same complete-release authorization when their conditions still match.\\nOtherwise obtain only the exact external authority that is missing.\\n\\n### Record the release decision, when required\\n\\n**Inputs:** The human's exact decision and unchanged release inputs.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only the selected release record (RLS) receives the\\n  permitted state and lifecycle-history entry.\\n- **Retained files:** The updated release record (RLS), retained through\\n  authorized Git actions.\\n- **Transient working material:** The applied result and current next action.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Compare the current release inputs with the human's reviewed inputs.\\n2. Preview the selected transition.\\n3. Apply the same command when its gates pass and effects match the decision.\\n4. Inspect the release record (RLS) and report the actual resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set RLS-ID=TARGET --decision RLS-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set RLS-ID=TARGET --decision RLS-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact RLS-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nFor rejection, add `--reason \\\"RLS-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both transition\\ncommands. Related verification records (VREC) and work orders (WO) are not\\ntransitioned automatically.\\n\\nFor a complete-release decision, use the existing `--reason` input to retain the\\nactual human decision reference and reviewed delivery-plan path and SHA-256.\\nThis is retained evidence of the supplied decision, not permission derived by\\nparsing arbitrary reason text. Preserve the referenced plan bytes for each later\\nauthority comparison. No new approval file or lifecycle state is needed.\\n\\n**Completion:** The release decision is recorded or explicitly refused.\\nNo Git tag, publication, or deployment has been performed by this transition.\\n\\n**Later use:** A released record may support [Confirm authority for the external action](DELIVER_RESULT.md#confirm-authority-for-the-external-action)'s external-action checks.\\nRejection follows the evaluator's remediation procedure.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"b9dc8aaeb4a404089d103f98731b5333d863f5934e462c9bb1c22ae5054e9376\"}]}]}\n2\t"
}

An ADR may be omitted only for a selected architecture whose accepted `decision_assessment` is `no_significant_decision`; every `adr_required` architecture needs at least one selected active ADR that decides it.\\n\\nAn architecture is applicable when it addresses an architecturally significant requirement implemented by this work order. ## Event 437: user text

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is a "Native hosted-workflow qualification" test (WO-HAG-009, VER-HAG-007). The task requires the agent to work independently in a disposable private hosted project to:
   - Inspect the existing `greeting()` function in `src/greeting.py` and prove it returns exactly `Hello rehearsal`
   - Author new lifecycle definitions (INT, CAP, REQ, SPEC, VER, WO, REL) for the greeting outcome
   - Exercise the complete lifecycle: draft → approve → start → implement → verify → release for test version 0.0.1
   - Record one test risk (RISK-P3-001) and its paired decision (DEC-P3-001) with mitigation choice
   - Demonstrate: out-of-scope handoff refusal, stale preview after draft change, uncertain-reply recovery, identical retry without duplicate effect, changed request under accepted key refusal
   - Use all seven configured read-only hag MCP tools on meaningful views
   - Export an earlier and later immutable snapshot into separate local destinations
   - Write `work/native-report.md` and `work/native-observations.json` with actual case outcomes
   - Synthetic actors: `test-owner` for definitions/work/decisions, `test-executor` for execution, `quality-owner` for verification, `release-owner` for release
   - Risk mitigation choice is `mitigate`, linked to authored work

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - SE Harness lifecycle workflow (schema v2): import → draft-open → create-artifact → revise-artifact → lifecycle transitions (approve/start/implement/verify/release)
   - Lifecycle v2 closed JSON schema with preview/apply mode (preview returns a digest, apply requires that digest)
   - Operation key idempotency (identical retry returns same result, changed request under same key is refused with HAG_REMOTE_KEY_REUSE)
   - Uncertain reply recovery (exit code 2, look up operation key to determine actual effect)
   - Draft validation (se-harness-draft-validation-v1 schema)
   - Artifact types: intent, capability, requirement, specification, verification, work_order, release_contract, risk, decision
   - Test-copy service with sandbox-projection authority mode
   - Evaluator version 0.22.1 (released), client version 0.22.2 (candidate)
   - Approved shell prefix for all helper commands (one command per Bash call, no pipelines)
   - Handoff evidence with file inputs within work order scope
   - Verification record (VREC) capture and release record (RLS) preparation
